Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The algebraic_geom operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 424. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 124 with 2 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_424(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 424. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^124(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any algebraic_geom module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 815. The projective resolution has length at most 715 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 815 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the algebraic_geom structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 715. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Proof: The recursion lemma estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 167 + O(1/n^754). Summing over all modes and applying the algebraic_geom recursion lemma criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 216.998 (Conservation Law)

Statement: For any algebraic_geom module M satisfying the conservation law hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 230. The projective resolution has length at most 873 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the conservation law decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_230(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 230. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^873(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.999 (Entropy Bound)

Statement: The algebraic_geom operator L associated with entropy bound theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 898. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 501 with 4 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: Consider the entropy bound operator L on the algebraic_geom space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 898 for all k. By the entropy bound theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 501. QED.

Theorem 216.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: In the algebraic_geom framework, every existence and uniqueness morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 218. The resulting operator achieves bound O(453/n^2).

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the existence and uniqueness decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_218(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 218. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^453(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the algebraic_geom framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 322. The resulting operator achieves bound O(407/n^1).

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 322 + O(1/n^407). Summing over all modes and applying the algebraic_geom convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.
